3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 70.13948pt\raise-24.66669pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-20.09326pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{C(\mathfrak{A}_{1})_{R}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.51198pt\raise-26.8125pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\varphi_{0}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.09326pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 44.09326pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A_{2R}\times A_{3R}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Thus,

\BAut(\mathcal{T})\subset\BAut(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\times\BAut(\mathfrak{A}_{2})\times\BAut(\mathfrak{A}_{3}).

Now, recall from
[
13
, §23.B]
that the map
\Int\colon\BGO(\mathfrak{A}_{i})\to\BAut(\mathfrak{A}_{i})
defines an isomorphism

\BPGO(\mathfrak{A}_{i})\xrightarrow{\sim}\BAut(\mathfrak{A}_{i})
. Therefore, we may
consider the inverse image of
\BAut(\mathcal{T})
under the surjective
morphism

\Int\colon\BGO(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\times\BGO(\mathfrak{A}_{2})\times\BGO(\mathfrak{A}_{3})\to\BAut(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\times\BAut(\mathfrak{A}_{2})\times\BAut(\mathfrak{A}_{3}).

Definition 3.3
.

The algebraic group scheme of
similitudes
of the trialitarian
triple
\mathcal{T}
is

\BGO(\mathcal{T})=\Int^{-1}\bigl(\BAut(\mathcal{T})\bigr)\subset\BGO(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\times\BGO(\mathfrak{A}_{2})\times\BGO(\mathfrak{A}_{3}).

From this definition, it follows that the map
\Int
restricts to a
surjective morphism (see
[
13
, (22.4)]
)

\Int\colon\BGO(\mathcal{T})\to\BAut(\mathcal{T}).

Its kernel is the algebraic group of
homotheties

\BHomot(\mathcal{T})=\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{m}}^{3}
, which lies in the center of
\BGO(\mathcal{T})
.
We may therefore consider the quotient

\BPGO(\mathcal{T})=\BGO(\mathcal{T})/\BHomot(\mathcal{T})\subset\BPGO(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\times\BPGO(\mathfrak{A}_{2})\times\BPGO(\mathfrak{A}_{3}),

and the map
\Int
yields an
isomorphism

\overline{\Int}\colon\BPGO(\mathcal{T})\xrightarrow{\sim}\BAut(\mathcal{T}).

Our goal in this subsection is to define a subgroup

\BOrth(\mathcal{T})\subset\BGO(\mathcal{T})
on the same model as the subgroup

\BOrth(\mathcal{C})
of the group
\BGO(\mathcal{C})
of similitudes of a
composition of quadratic spaces, so that when
\mathcal{T}=\End(\mathcal{C})
for
some
composition
\mathcal{C}
of quadratic spaces of dimension
8

we may identify

\BOrth(\mathcal{T})=\BOrth(\mathcal{C}),\qquad\BGO(\mathcal{T})=\BGO(\mathcal{C})\quad\text{and}\quad\BPGO(\mathcal{T})=\BPGO(\mathcal{C});

see Proposition
3.10
. Moreover, for an arbitrary
trialitarian triple
\mathcal{T}
, we
relate
\BGO(\mathcal{T})
to the extended Clifford group
\BOmega(\mathfrak{A}_{1})
to
obtain canonical isomorphisms

\BSpin(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\xrightarrow{\sim}\BOrth(\mathcal{T})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\BPGO(\mathcal{T})\xrightarrow{\sim}\BPGO^{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{1}),

see Theorems
3.4
and
3.12
.

A key tool is the following construction: let

\varphi_{+}\colon\mathfrak{C}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\to\mathfrak{A}_{2}
and

\varphi_{-}\colon\mathfrak{C}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\to\mathfrak{A}_{3}
be the two components of the
isomorphism
\varphi_{0}\colon\mathfrak{C}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\to\mathfrak{A}_{2}\times\mathfrak{A}_{3}
, which
is part of the structure of
\mathcal{T}
. Recall
from (
1.22
) that
\varphi_{+}
and
\varphi_{-}

restrict to morphisms

\varphi_{+}\colon\BOmega(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\to\BGO^{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{2})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\varphi_{-}\colon\BOmega(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\to\BGO^{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{3}).

Combine
\varphi_{+}
and
\varphi_{-}
with the morphism

\chi_{0}\colon\BOmega(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\to\BGO^{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})
of §
1.3

to obtain a morphism

(3.6)

\psi_{\mathcal{T}}\colon\BOmega(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\to\BGO(\mathcal{T})

as follows: for every commutative
F
-algebra
R
and

\xi\in\BOmega(\mathfrak{A}_{1})(R)
, let

\psi_{\mathcal{T}}(\xi)=\bigl(\chi_{0}(\xi),\,\varphi_{+}(\xi),\,\varphi_{-}(\xi)\bigr)\in\BGO^{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})(R)\times\BGO^{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{2})(R)\times\BGO^{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{3})(R).

Proposition
1.7
shows that

C\bigl(\Int(\chi_{0}(\xi))\bigr)=\Int(\xi)
, hence

\varphi_{0}\circ C\bigl(\Int(\chi_{0}(\xi))\bigr)\circ\varphi_{0}^{-1}=\Int\bigl(\varphi_{0}(\xi)\bigr)=\Int\bigl(\varphi_{+}(\xi)\bigr)\times\Int\bigl(\varphi_{-}(\xi)\bigr),

which means that
\bigl(\Int(\chi_{0}(\xi)),\,\Int(\varphi_{+}(\xi)),\,\Int(\varphi_{-}(\xi))\bigr)
lies in
\BAut(\mathcal{T})(R)
, and therefore

\psi_{\mathcal{T}}(\xi)\in\BGO(\mathcal{T})(R)
. Note that
\psi_{\mathcal{T}}
is injective,
since
\bigl(\varphi_{+}(\xi),\,\varphi_{-}(\xi)\bigr)=\varphi_{0}(\xi)
and

\varphi_{0}
is an isomorphism.

We first use the map
\psi_{\mathcal{T}}
to prove:

Theorem 3.4
.

Projection on the first component
\pi_{\mathcal{T}}\colon\BPGO(\mathcal{T})\to\BPGO(\mathfrak{A}_{1})
defines an isomorphism

\BPGO(\mathcal{T})\xrightarrow{\sim}\BPGO^{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{1}).

Proof.

Let
R
be a commutative
F
-algebra and

(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3})\in\BAut(\mathcal{T})(R)
. Since
\varphi_{0}
is
an isomorphism,
\gamma_{2}
and
\gamma_{3}
are uniquely determined by

\gamma_{1}
and commutativity of the
diagram (
3.5
). Therefore,
\pi_{\mathcal{T}}
is
injective. Moreover, commutativity of the
diagram (
3.5
) shows that
C(\gamma_{1})
leaves the
center of
C(\mathfrak{A}_{1})
fixed, which means that
\gamma_{1}
lies in the
connected component of the identity
\BAut^{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})(R)
. Therefore,
the image of
\pi_{\mathcal{T}}
lies in
\BPGO^{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})
.

To complete the proof, we show that
\pi_{\mathcal{T}}
is surjective on

\BPGO^{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})
. Since
\BPGO^{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})
is smooth, it suffices to
consider rational points over an algebraic closure
F_{\text{\rm alg}}
of
F
,
by
[
13
, (22.3)]
. Recall from
Proposition
1.8
that
\chi_{0}
is surjective. For
every

g_{1}\in\BGO^{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})(F_{\text{\rm alg}})
, we may therefore find

\xi\in\BOmega(\mathfrak{A}_{1})(F_{\text{\rm alg}})
such that
\chi_{0}(\xi)=g_{1}
. Then

\psi_{\mathcal{T}}(\xi)\in\BGO(\mathcal{T})(F_{\text{\rm alg}})
, and its image

\overline{\psi}_{\mathcal{T}}(\xi)
in
\BPGO(\mathcal{T})(F_{\text{\rm alg}})
satisfies

\pi_{\mathcal{T}}\bigl(\overline{\psi}_{\mathcal{T}}(\xi)\bigr)=g_{1}F_{\text{\rm alg}}^{\times}
.
We have thus found an element in

\BPGO(\mathcal{T})(F_{\text{\rm alg}})
that maps under
\pi_{\mathcal{T}}
to any given

g_{1}F_{\text{\rm alg}}^{\times}\in\BPGO^{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})(F_{\text{\rm alg}})
, hence
\pi_{\mathcal{T}}
is
surjective.
∎

Corollary 3.5
.

The algebraic group schemes
\BGO(\mathcal{T})
and
\BPGO(\mathcal{T})
are smooth
and connected.

Proof.

That
\BPGO(\mathcal{T})
is smooth and connected readily follows from the
theorem, since
\BPGO^{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})
is smooth and connected
by
[
13
, §23.B]
. Then
\BGO(\mathcal{T})
is also smooth and connected
because
\BPGO(\mathcal{T})=\BGO(\mathcal{T})/\BHomot(\mathcal{T})
with
\BHomot(\mathcal{T})

smooth and connected, see
[
13
, (22.12)]
.
∎

We next use
\psi_{\mathcal{T}}
to determine the structure of
\BGO(\mathcal{T})
. Let
Z_{1}\simeq F\times F
denote the center of
C(\mathfrak{A}_{1})
, and recall
that
R_{Z_{1}/F}(\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{m}})
lies in the center of
\BOmega(\mathfrak{A}_{1})

(see (
1.4
)). For every commutative
F
-algebra
R

and
z\in(Z_{1})_{R}^{\times}
, Proposition
1.7
yields

\chi_{0}(z)=N_{Z_{1}/F}(z)
, while
\varphi_{+}(z)
,
\varphi_{-}(z)\in R^{\times}
. Therefore,

(3.7)

\psi_{\mathcal{T}}(z)=(N_{Z_{1}/F}(z),\,\varphi_{+}(z),\,\varphi_{-}(z)\bigr)\in R^{\times}\times R^{\times}\times R^{\times}=\BHomot(\mathcal{T})(R).

Proposition 3.6
.

The morphism
\psi_{\mathcal{T}}
and the inclusion
i\colon\BHomot(\mathcal{T})\to\BGO(\mathcal{T})
define an isomorphism

\overline{\psi_{\mathcal{T}}\times i}\colon\bigl(\BOmega(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\times\BHomot(\mathcal{T})\bigr)/R_{Z_{1}/F}(\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{m}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\BGO(\mathcal{T}),

where
R_{Z_{1}/F}(\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{m}})
is embedded into the product canonically in
the first factor and by the inversion followed by
\psi_{\mathcal{T}}
in the
second (so the copies of
R_{Z_{1}/F}(\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{m}})
in
\BOmega(\mathfrak{A}_{1})
and
in
\BHomot(\mathcal{T})
are identified in the quotient).

Proof.

It is clear from the definition of the quotient that the morphism

\overline{\psi_{\mathcal{T}}\times i}
is defined.
To prove that it is injective, consider an arbitrary commutative

F
-algebra
R
and pick
\xi\in\BOmega(\mathfrak{A}_{1})(R)
and

\nu=(\nu_{1},\nu_{2},\nu_{3})\in\BHomot(\mathcal{T})(R)
such that

\psi_{\mathcal{T}}(\xi)\cdot\nu=1
in
\BGO(\mathcal{T})(R)
, i.e.,

\chi_{0}(\xi)=\nu_{1}^{-1},\qquad\varphi_{+}(\xi)=\nu_{2}^{-1}\quad\text{and}\quad\varphi_{-}(\xi)=\nu_{3}^{-1}.

The last two equations show that
\varphi_{0}(\xi)=(\nu_{2}^{-1},\nu_{3}^{-1})
in
(A_{2})_{R}\times(A_{3})_{R}
. Since
\varphi_{0}
is an
isomorphism, it follows that
\xi
lies in

(Z_{1})_{R}^{\times}
, hence
(\xi,\nu)
is trivial in the quotient,
for
\nu=\psi_{\mathcal{T}}(\xi)^{-1}
.

To complete the proof, it remains to show that

\overline{\psi_{\mathcal{T}}\times i}
is
surjective. Since
\BGO(\mathcal{T})
is smooth by
Corollary
3.5
, it suffices to consider the groups
of rational points over an algebraic closure
F_{\text{\rm alg}}
of
F
. Let

g=(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})\in\BGO(\mathcal{T})(F_{\text{\rm alg}})
. Note that
g_{1}
,
g_{2}
and

g_{3}
are proper similitudes, because
\BGO(\mathcal{T})
is connected by
Corollary
3.5
. We know from
Proposition
1.8
that
\chi_{0}\colon\BOmega(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\to\BGO^{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})
is surjective, hence we may find

\xi\in\BOmega(\mathfrak{A}_{1})(F_{\text{\rm alg}})
such that
\chi_{0}(\xi)=g_{1}
. Then

\psi_{\mathcal{T}}(\xi)=(g_{1},g^{\prime}_{2},g^{\prime}_{3})
for some

g^{\prime}_{2}\in\BGO^{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{2})(F_{\text{\rm alg}})
and

g^{\prime}_{3}\in\BGO^{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{3})(F_{\text{\rm alg}})
. As
g
and
\psi_{\mathcal{T}}(\xi)
belong to

\BGO(\mathcal{T})(F_{\text{\rm alg}})
, the following diagrams commute:

\textstyle{C(\mathfrak{A}_{1})_{F_{\text{\rm alg}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{0}}
\scriptstyle{C(\Int(g_{1}))}
\textstyle{(A_{2})_{F_{\text{\rm alg}}}\times(A_{3})_{F_{\text{\rm alg}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Int(g_{2})\times\Int(g_{3})}
\textstyle{C(\mathfrak{A}_{1})_{F_{\text{\rm alg}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{0}}
\textstyle{(A_{2})_{F_{\text{\rm alg}}}\times(A_{3})_{F_{\text{\rm alg}}}}

\textstyle{C(\mathfrak{A}_{1})_{F_{\text{\rm alg}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{0}}
\scriptstyle{C(\Int(g_{1}))}
\textstyle{(A_{2})_{F_{\text{\rm alg}}}\times(A_{3})_{F_{\text{\rm alg}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Int(g^{\prime}_{2})\times\Int(g^{\prime}_{3})}
\textstyle{C(\mathfrak{A}_{1})_{F_{\text{\rm alg}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varphi_{0}}
\textstyle{(A_{2})_{F_{\text{\rm alg}}}\times(A_{3})_{F_{\text{\rm alg}}}}

Therefore,
\Int(g_{2})=\Int(g^{\prime}_{2})
and
\Int(g_{3})=\Int(g^{\prime}_{3})
, which
implies that
g_{2}=g^{\prime}_{2}\nu_{2}
and
g_{3}=g^{\prime}_{3}\nu_{3}
for some
\nu_{2}
,

\nu_{3}\in F_{\text{\rm alg}}^{\times}
. With

\nu=(1,\nu_{2},\nu_{3})\in\BHomot(\mathcal{T})(F_{\text{\rm alg}})
we then have

\psi_{\mathcal{T}}(\xi)\cdot\nu=(g_{1},g^{\prime}_{2}\nu_{2},g^{\prime}_{3}\nu_{3})=(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})=g.

Surjectivity of
\overline{\psi_{\mathcal{T}}\times i}
follows.
∎

Corollary 3.7
.

Let
m\colon\BHomot(\mathcal{T})\to\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{m}}
denote the multiplication map
carrying
(\nu_{1},\nu_{2},\nu_{3})
to
\nu_{1}\nu_{2}\nu_{3}
. There is a
morphism
\rho_{\mathcal{T}}\colon\BGO(\mathcal{T})\to\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{m}}
uniquely determined by
the condition that the following diagram commutes:

(3.8)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 32.92361pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-32.92361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\BOmega(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\times\BHomot(\mathcal{T})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 55.62pt\raise 6.1111pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\psi_{\mathcal{T}}\times i}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 103.9791pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 2.44067pt\raise-22.3125pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(\mu\circ\chi_{0})\times m}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 56.92361pt\raise-26.31146pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 65.45135pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 103.9791pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\BGO(\mathcal{T})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 92.61325pt\raise-20.99998pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rho_{\mathcal{T}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 79.9791pt\raise-24.98306pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-31.62502pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 56.92361pt\raise-31.62502pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{m}}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 120.31934pt\raise-31.62502pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Proof.

Proposition
3.6
identifies
\BGO(\mathcal{T})
with a quotient
of
\BOmega(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\times\BHomot(\mathcal{T})
by
R_{Z_{1}/F}(\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{m}})
, hence
to prove the existence and uniqueness of
\rho_{\mathcal{T}}
it suffices to
show that
(\mu\circ\chi_{0})
and
m
coincide on the images of

R_{Z_{1}/F}(\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{m}})
in
\BOmega(\mathcal{T})
by inclusion and in

\BHomot(\mathcal{T})
by
\psi_{\mathcal{T}}
.
For every commutative
F
-algebra
R
and

z\in(Z_{1})_{R}^{\times}

we have
\chi_{0}(z)=N_{Z_{1}/F}(z)
by
Proposition
1.7
, hence

(\mu\circ\chi_{0})(z)=N_{Z_{1}/F}(z)^{2}
. On the other hand

N_{Z_{1}/F}(z)=\varphi_{+}(z)\varphi_{-}(z)
, hence

(m\circ\psi_{\mathcal{T}})(z)=m\bigl(N_{Z_{1}/F}(z),\,\varphi_{+}(z),\,\varphi_{-}(z)\bigr)=N_{Z_{1}/F}(z)^{2}.

Thus,
(\mu\circ\chi_{0})
and
m
coincide on the images of

R_{Z_{1}/F}(\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{m}})
.
∎

Definition 3.8
.

A morphism

\lambda_{\mathcal{T}}\colon\BGO(\mathcal{T})\to\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{m}}^{3}
is defined as follows:
for every commutative
F
-algebra
R
and

g=(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})\in\BGO(\mathcal{T})(R)
, set

\lambda_{\mathcal{T}}(g)=\bigl(\rho_{\mathcal{T}}(g)\mu(g_{1})^{-1},\,\rho_{\mathcal{T}}(g)\mu(g_{2})^{-1},\,\rho_{\mathcal{T}}(g)\mu(g_{3})^{-1}\bigr)\in R^{\times}\times R^{\times}\times R^{\times}.

From the definition of
\rho_{\mathcal{T}}
, it follows that for

\nu=(\nu_{1},\nu_{2},\nu_{3})\in\BHomot(\mathcal{T})(R)

\rho_{\mathcal{T}}(\nu)=m(\nu)=\nu_{1}\nu_{2}\nu_{3},

hence

(3.9)

\lambda_{\mathcal{T}}(\nu)=(\nu_{2}\nu_{3}\nu_{1}^{-1},\,\nu_{3}\nu_{1}\nu_{2}^{-1},\,\nu_{1}\nu_{2}\nu_{3}^{-1}).

The definition of
\rho_{\mathcal{T}}
also yields

\rho_{\mathcal{T}}\bigl(\psi_{\mathcal{T}}(\xi)\bigr)=\mu\bigl(\chi_{0}(\xi)\bigr)
for

\xi\in\BOmega(\mathfrak{A}_{1})(R)
. Letting

\underline{\mu}\colon\BOmega(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\to R_{Z_{1}/F}(\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{m}})
denote the
multiplier map, we have by Proposition
1.7

\mu\bigl(\chi_{0}(\xi)\bigr)=N_{Z_{1}/F}\bigl(\underline{\mu}(\xi)\bigr)=\varphi_{+}\bigl(\underline{\mu}(\xi)\bigr)\cdot\varphi_{-}\bigl(\underline{\mu}(\xi)\bigr).

As
\varphi_{0}
is an isomorphism of algebras with quadratic pair, we
also have

(3.10)

\bigl(\varphi_{+}(\underline{\mu}(\xi)),\,\varphi_{-}(\underline{\mu}(\xi))\bigr)=\varphi_{0}\bigl(\underline{\mu}(\xi)\bigr)=\bigl(\mu(\varphi_{+}(\xi)),\,\mu(\varphi_{-}(\xi))\bigr).

Therefore, the definition of
\lambda_{\mathcal{T}}
yields

(3.11)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\mathcal{T}}\bigl(\psi_{\mathcal{T}}(\xi)\bigr)

\displaystyle=\bigl(1,\,\mu\bigl(\chi_{0}(\xi)\bigr)\mu\bigl(\varphi_{+}(\xi)\bigr)^{-1},\,\mu\bigl(\chi_{0}(\xi)\bigr)\mu\bigl(\varphi_{-}(\xi)\bigr)^{-1}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\bigl(1,\,\mu\bigl(\varphi_{-}(\xi)\bigr),\,\mu\bigl(\varphi_{+}(\xi)\bigr)\bigr).

Definition 3.9
.

Let

\BOrth(\mathcal{T})=\ker(\lambda_{\mathcal{T}}\colon\BGO(\mathcal{T})\to\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{m}}^{3}).

As in the proof of Proposition
2.18
, it follows
from (
3.9
)
that the map
\lambda_{\mathcal{T}}\colon\BHomot(\mathcal{T})\to\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{m}}^{3}
, hence also

\lambda_{\mathcal{T}}\colon\BGO(\mathcal{T})\to\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{m}}^{3}
, is surjective. Therefore, the
following sequence is exact:

1\to\BOrth(\mathcal{T})\to\BGO(\mathcal{T})\xrightarrow{\lambda_{\mathcal{T}}}\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{m}}^{3}\to 1

Now, let
\BZO(\mathcal{T})
be the kernel of the canonical map

\BOrth(\mathcal{T})\to\BPGO(\mathcal{T})
, which is the composition of the
inclusion
\BOrth(\mathcal{T})\subset\BGO(\mathcal{T})
and the canonical
epimorphism
\BGO(\mathcal{T})\to\BPGO(\mathcal{T})
. Thus, letting
m
be the
multiplication map
(\nu_{1},\nu_{2},\nu_{3})\mapsto\nu_{1}\nu_{2}\nu_{3}
,

\BZO(\mathcal{T})=\BHomot(\mathcal{T})\cap\BOrth(\mathcal{T})=\ker(m\colon\boldsymbol{\mu}_{2}\times\boldsymbol{\mu}_{2}\times\boldsymbol{\mu}_{2}\to\boldsymbol{\mu}_{2})\simeq\boldsymbol{\mu}_{2}\times\boldsymbol{\mu}_{2}.

The same arguments as in Proposition
2.19
yield the
following commutative diagram with exact rows and columns:

(3.12)

0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.49998pt\raise-120.64pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 91.31247pt\raise-95.79222pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{m}}^{3}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 102.84021pt\raise-120.64pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 166.45122pt\raise-95.79222pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 219.22198pt\raise-95.79222pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-127.58444pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 38.99998pt\raise-127.58444pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{1}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 97.34021pt\raise-127.58444pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{1}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 166.45122pt\raise-127.58444pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 219.22198pt\raise-127.58444pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Now, we show that the definitions above are compatible with the
corresponding definitions for compositions of quadratic spaces
in §
2.3
.

Proposition 3.10
.

For
\mathcal{C}
any composition of quadratic spaces of dimension
8
and

\mathcal{T}=\End(\mathcal{C})
,
canonical isomorphisms yield identifications

\BHomot(\mathcal{C})=\BHomot(\mathcal{T}),\quad\BOrth(\mathcal{C})=\BOrth(\mathcal{T}),\quad\BGO(\mathcal{C})=\BGO(\mathcal{T}),\quad\BPGO(\mathcal{C})=\BPGO(\mathcal{T}).

Moreover, the following diagram commutes:

(3.13)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 19.34024pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-19.34024pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\BGO(\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-16.0348pt\raise-16.13223pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.93056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\lambda_{\mathcal{C}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.67998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 43.34024pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\BGO(\mathcal{T})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 62.68048pt\raise-16.13223pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.93056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\lambda_{\mathcal{T}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 62.68048pt\raise-23.67998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-11.52774pt\raise-32.26447pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{m}}^{3}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\kern 51.15274pt\raise-32.26447pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{m}}^{3}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Proof.

Let
R
be a commutative
F
-algebra. For every

(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},\lambda_{3})\in\BGO(\mathcal{C})(R)
the triple

(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})
lies in
\BGO(\mathcal{T})(R)
, as seen in the first part of
the proof of Proposition
3.1
. Since
\lambda_{3}
is
uniquely
determined by
g_{1}
,
g_{2}
and
g_{3}
, mapping

(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},\lambda_{3})
to
(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})
defines an injective
map
\BGO(\mathcal{C})\to\BGO(\mathcal{T})
.
Proposition
3.1
also shows that for
F_{\text{\rm alg}}
an
algebraic closure of
F
the map
\BGO(\mathcal{C})(F_{\text{\rm alg}})\to\BGO(\mathcal{T})(F_{\text{\rm alg}})
is surjective. This is
sufficient to prove that the map
\BGO(\mathcal{C})\to\BGO(\mathcal{T})
is surjective, since

\BGO(\mathcal{T})
is smooth by
Corollary
3.5
. We have thus obtained
a canonical isomorphism
\BGO(\mathcal{C})\xrightarrow{\sim}\BGO(\mathcal{T})
. This isomorphism maps
\BHomot(\mathcal{C})

to
\BHomot(\mathcal{T})
, hence it induces an isomorphism

\BPGO(\mathcal{C})\xrightarrow{\sim}\BPGO(\mathcal{T})
.

In order to prove that the isomorphism
\BGO(\mathcal{C})=\BGO(\mathcal{T})

also maps
\BOrth(\mathcal{C})

to
\BOrth(\mathcal{T})
, it suffices to prove that the
diagram (
3.13
) is commutative. For this, we use
the description of
\BGO(\mathcal{T})
in Proposition
3.6
as a
quotient of the product of
\BOmega(\mathfrak{A}_{1})
and
\BHomot(\mathcal{T})
. It
is clear from (
3.9
) that
\lambda_{\mathcal{C}}
and

\lambda_{\mathcal{T}}
coincide on the image of

\BHomot(\mathcal{C})=\BHomot(\mathcal{T})
in
\BGO(\mathcal{C})=\BGO(\mathcal{T})
. Therefore,
it suffices to consider the image of
\BOmega(\mathfrak{A}_{1})
under

\psi_{\mathcal{T}}
.

Let

\mathcal{C}=\bigl((V_{1},q_{1}),\,(V_{2},q_{2}),\,(V_{3},q_{3}),\,*_{3}\bigr)
, so

\varphi_{0}=C_{0}(\alpha)
,

A_{1}=\End V_{1}
and

\BOmega(\mathfrak{A}_{1})=\BOmega(q_{1})
.
Let
R
be a commutative
F
-algebra and
let
\xi\in\BOmega(q_{1})(R)
.
To simplify notation, write
g_{1}=\chi_{0}(\xi)\in\BGO^{+}(q_{1})(R)
,

g_{2}=C_{+}(\alpha)(\xi)\in\BGO^{+}(q_{2})(R)

and
g_{3}=C_{-}(\alpha)(\xi)\in\BGO^{+}(q_{3})(R)
, so

\psi_{\mathcal{T}}(\xi)=(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3}).

Now, (
3.10
) yields

C_{0}(\alpha)\bigl(\underline{\mu}(\xi)\bigr)=\bigl(\mu(g_{2}),\mu(g_{3})\bigr)
, and by (
1.2
), we
have
g_{1}(x_{1})=\iota\bigl(\underline{\mu}(\xi)\bigr)\xi x_{1}\xi^{-1}

for every
x_{1}\in V_{1R}
. By taking the image of each side of the
last equation under
C(\alpha)
, we obtain

\begin{pmatrix}0&r_{g_{1}(x_{1})}\\
\ell_{g_{1}(x_{1})}&0\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\mu(g_{3})&0\\
0&\mu(g_{2})\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}g_{2}&0\\
0&g_{3}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}0&r_{x_{1}}\\
\ell_{x_{1}}&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}g_{2}^{-1}&0\\
0&g_{3}^{-1}\end{pmatrix}.

This equation yields

r_{g_{1}(x_{1})}g_{3}=\mu(g_{3})g_{2}r_{x_{1}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\ell_{g_{1}(x_{1})}g_{2}=\mu(g_{2})g_{3}\ell_{x_{1}}\qquad\text{for all
$x_{1}\in V_{1R}$},

which means that for all
x_{1}\in V_{1R}
,
x_{2}\in V_{2R}
and

x_{3}\in V_{3R}

g_{3}(x_{3})*_{2}g_{1}(x_{1})=\mu(g_{3})g_{2}(x_{3}*_{2}x_{1})\qquad\text{and}\qquad g_{1}(x_{1})*_{3}g_{2}(x_{2})=\mu(g_{2})g_{3}(x_{1}*_{3}x_{2}).

These equations show that

\bigl(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3},\mu(g_{2})\bigr)\in\BGO(\mathcal{C})(R)
, hence
by (
2.25
)

\lambda_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\psi_{\mathcal{T}}(\xi)\bigr)=\bigl(1,\mu(g_{3}),\mu(g_{2})\bigr).

Therefore,
\lambda_{\mathcal{C}}\bigl(\psi_{\mathcal{T}}(\xi)\bigr)=\lambda_{\mathcal{T}}\bigl(\psi_{\mathcal{T}}(\xi)\bigr)
by (
3.11
), and
the proof is complete.
∎

Corollary 3.11
.

For every trialitarian triple
\mathcal{T}
, the algebraic group scheme

\BOrth(\mathcal{T})
is smooth.

Proof.

Over an algebraic closure
F_{\text{\rm alg}}
of
F
the trialitarian
triple
\mathcal{T}
is split, hence by Theorem
3.2
we may
find a composition
\mathcal{C}
of
quadratic spaces of dimension
8
over
F_{\text{\rm alg}}
such that

\mathcal{T}_{F_{\text{\rm alg}}}\simeq\End(\mathcal{C})
. Then

\BOrth(\mathcal{T}_{F_{\text{\rm alg}}})
is isomorphic to
\BOrth(\mathcal{C})
,
which is smooth by Proposition
2.18
, hence

\BOrth(\mathcal{T})
is smooth by
[
13
, (21.10)]
.
∎

The final result in this subsection elucidates the structure of

\BOrth(\mathcal{T})
.

Theorem 3.12
.

For every trialitarian triple
\mathcal{T}
, the morphism
\psi_{\mathcal{T}}

restricts to an isomorphism

\psi_{\mathcal{T}}\colon\BSpin(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\xrightarrow{\sim}\BOrth(\mathcal{T}).

Proof.

When defining
\psi_{\mathcal{T}}
, we already observed that this morphism is
injective.
Recall from §
1.3
that
\BSpin(\mathfrak{A}_{1})
is the kernel
of
\underline{\mu}\colon\BOmega(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\to R_{Z_{1}/F}(\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{m}})
. Therefore, (
3.10
) and
(
3.11
) show that
\psi_{\mathcal{T}}
map
\BSpin(\mathfrak{A}_{1})
to

\BOrth(\mathcal{T})
.

To prove that
\psi_{\mathcal{T}}
maps
\BSpin(\mathfrak{A}_{1})
onto
\BOrth(\mathcal{T})
,
it suffices to consider the groups of rational points over an
algebraic closure
F_{\text{\rm alg}}
of
F
, because we know by
Corollary
3.11
that
\BOrth(\mathcal{T})
is
smooth. Proposition
3.6
shows that
\psi_{\mathcal{T}}\times i\colon\BOmega(\mathcal{T})(F_{\text{\rm alg}})\times\BHomot(\mathcal{T})(F_{\text{\rm alg}})\to\BGO(\mathcal{T})(F_{\text{\rm alg}})
is surjective, hence for any

g\in\BOrth(\mathcal{T})(F_{\text{\rm alg}})
we may find
\xi\in\BOmega(\mathcal{T})(F_{\text{\rm alg}})
and

\nu=(\nu_{1},\nu_{2},\nu_{3})\in\BHomot(\mathcal{T})(F_{\text{\rm alg}})
such that

\psi_{\mathcal{T}}(\xi)\cdot\nu=g
. Taking the image of each side under

\lambda_{\mathcal{T}}
and using (
3.9
)
and (
3.11
), we obtain

(1,\lambda_{2},\lambda_{3})\cdot(\nu_{2}\nu_{3}\nu_{1}^{-1},\,\nu_{3}\nu_{1}\nu_{2}^{-1},\,\nu_{1}\nu_{2}\nu_{3}^{-1})=(1,1,1)

for some
\lambda_{2}
,
\lambda_{3}\in F_{\text{\rm alg}}^{\times}
, hence

\nu_{1}=\nu_{2}\nu_{3}
. Therefore,
\nu=\psi_{\mathcal{T}}(z)
for

z\in(Z_{1})^{\times}_{F_{\text{\rm alg}}}
such that
\varphi_{0}(z)=(\nu_{2},\nu_{3})
,
and
\psi_{\mathcal{T}}(\xi z)=g
. Since
\lambda_{\mathcal{T}}(g)=(1,1,1)
,
(
3.10
) and (
3.11
) show that

\underline{\mu}(\xi z)=1
, hence
\xi z\in\BSpin(\mathfrak{A}_{1})(F_{\text{\rm alg}})
. Thus,
\psi_{\mathcal{T}}
maps
\BSpin(\mathfrak{A}_{1})

onto
\BOrth(\mathcal{T})
.
∎

Corollary 3.13
.

The following diagram, in which the vertical maps are isomorphisms,
is commutative with exact rows:

(3.14)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern Proof.

The upper sequence is (
1.5
), and the lower
sequence is from (

). Commutativity of the right
square follows from the definition of
\chi^{\prime}
as the composition of

\chi_{0}
with the canonical map
\BGO^{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\to\BPGO^{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})
,
and bijectivity of the vertical maps is proved in
Theorems
3.4
and
3.12
.
∎

3.3.
Derived trialitarian triples

To every trialitarian triple
\mathcal{T}=(\mathfrak{A}_{1},\mathfrak{A}_{2},\mathfrak{A}_{3},\varphi_{0})
we
attach in this subsection two derived trialitarian triples

\partial\mathcal{T}=(\mathfrak{A}_{2},\mathfrak{A}_{3},\mathfrak{A}_{1},\varphi^{\prime}_{0})\quad\text{and}\quad\partial^{2}\mathcal{T}=(\mathfrak{A}_{3},\mathfrak{A}_{1},\mathfrak{A}_{2},\varphi^{\prime\prime}_{0})

in such a way that for every composition
\mathcal{C}
of quadratic spaces
of dimension
8

\partial\End(\mathcal{C})=\End(\partial\mathcal{C})\quad\text{and}\quad\partial^{2}\End(\mathcal{C})=\End(\partial^{2}\mathcal{C}).

The two components of the isomorphisms

\varphi_{0}^{\prime}\colon\mathfrak{C}(\mathfrak{A}_{2})\to\mathfrak{A}_{3}\times\mathfrak{A}_{1}\quad\text{and}\quad\varphi_{0}^{\prime\prime}\colon\mathfrak{C}(\mathfrak{A}_{3})\to\mathfrak{A}_{1}\times\mathfrak{A}_{2}

are determined as lifts (in the sense of Definition
1.20
)
of Lie algebra homomorphisms

\theta^{\prime}_{+}\colon\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathfrak{A}_{2})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathfrak{A}_{3})\quad\text{and}\quad\theta^{\prime\prime}_{+}\colon\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathfrak{A}_{3})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathfrak{A}_{1}),

\theta^{\prime}_{-}\colon\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathfrak{A}_{2})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\quad\text{and}\quad\theta^{\prime\prime}_{-}\colon\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathfrak{A}_{3})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathfrak{A}_{2}).

Our main result is the following:

Theorem 3.14
.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(\mathfrak{A}_{1},\mathfrak{A}_{2},\mathfrak{A}_{3},\varphi_{0})
be a trialitarian triple
over an arbitrary field
F
, and let

\theta_{+}\colon\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathfrak{A}_{2})\quad\text{and}\quad\theta_{-}\colon\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathfrak{A}_{3})

denote the Lie algebra homomorphisms induced (as per
Definition
1.20
) by the two components of
\varphi_{0}
:

\varphi_{+}\colon\mathfrak{C}_{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\to\mathfrak{A}_{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\varphi_{-}\colon\mathfrak{C}_{-}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\to\mathfrak{A}_{3}.

The homomorphisms
\theta_{+}
and
\theta_{-}
are isomorphisms, and
the following Lie algebra homomorphisms are liftable:

\theta^{\prime}_{+}=\theta_{-}\circ\theta_{+}^{-1},\qquad\theta^{\prime}_{-}=\theta_{+}^{-1},\qquad\theta^{\prime\prime}_{+}=\theta_{-}^{-1},\qquad\theta^{\prime\prime}_{-}=\theta_{+}\circ\theta_{-}^{-1}.

Moreover,
\theta^{\prime}_{+}
and
\theta^{\prime}_{-}
on one side, and
\theta^{\prime\prime}_{+}

and
\theta^{\prime\prime}_{-}
on the other side, are of opposite signs (see
Definition
1.20
).

Corollary
1.22
shows that we may extend scalars to a
Galois extension of
F
in order to show that a Lie algebra
homomorphism is liftable. We may thus reduce to split trialitarian
triples, i.e., triples of the form
\End(\mathcal{C})
. We investigate this
case first. The proof of Theorem
3.14
will quickly
follow after (
3.18
).

Let
\mathcal{C}=\bigl((V_{1},q_{1}),\,(V_{2},q_{2}),\,(V_{3},q_{3}),\,*_{3}\bigr)
be a
composition of quadratic spaces of dimension
8
over an arbitrary
field
F
. Recall from Proposition
2.20
that the Lie
algebra
\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathcal{C})
can be described as a subalgebra of

\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{1})\times\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{2})\times\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{3})
. Let

\pi_{1}\colon\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{1}),\qquad\pi_{2}\colon\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{2}),\qquad\pi_{3}\colon\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{3})

denote the projections on the three components, and let

\theta_{+}\colon\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{1})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{2})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\theta_{-}\colon\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{1})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{3})

be the Lie algebra homomorphisms induced by the two components of

C_{0}(\alpha)
,

C_{+}(\alpha)\colon C_{+}(V_{1},q_{1})\to\End(V_{2})\qquad\text{and}\qquad C_{-}(\alpha)\colon C_{-}(V_{1},q_{1})\to\End(V_{3}).

Lemma 3.15
.

The following diagram, where all the maps are isomorphisms, is
commutative:

(3.15)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 19.32121pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-19.32121pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 54.91844pt\raise 6.34723pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.51387pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\theta_{+}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 105.96364pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 18.65106pt\raise-48.95233pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.93056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\theta_{-}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 57.09528pt\raise-81.66669pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 59.64243pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 105.96364pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{2})}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-31.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 44.36461pt\raise-31.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 27.74762pt\raise-10.96529pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.78854pt\raise-8.05554pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 82.9581pt\raise-10.96529pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{2}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 109.48845pt\raise-8.05554pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.06328pt\raise-55.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi_{3}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 62.64243pt\raise-81.66669pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 122.28485pt\raise-31.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-60.7778pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 59.64243pt\raise-60.7778pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 122.28485pt\raise-60.7778pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-89.61115pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 43.32121pt\raise-89.61115pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{3})}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 122.28485pt\raise-89.61115pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Proof.

First, observe that
\pi_{1}
is the differential of the morphism

\pi_{\End(\mathcal{C})}
under the identification
\BPGO(\mathcal{C})=\BPGO\bigl(\End(\mathcal{C})\bigr)
of
Proposition
3.10
. The morphism
\pi_{\End(\mathcal{C})}

is an isomorphism by Theorem
3.4
, hence
\pi_{1}
is an
isomorphism. Similarly,
\pi_{2}
is the differential of the
isomorphism obtained by the composition

\BPGO(\mathcal{C})\xrightarrow{\partial}\BPGO(\partial\mathcal{C})\xrightarrow{\pi_{\End(\partial\mathcal{C})}}\BPGO^{+}(q_{2}),

hence
\pi_{2}
is an isomorphism. Likewise,
\pi_{3}
is an
isomorphism.

Now, recall from (
1.23
) that
\theta_{+}
and

\theta_{-}
are defined by the following commutative diagrams, where

\overline{C_{+}(\alpha)}
and
\overline{C_{-}(\alpha)}
are obtained
by composing
C_{+}(\alpha)
and
C_{-}(\alpha)
with the canonical
homomorphisms
\mathfrak{go}(q_{2})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{2})
or
\mathfrak{go}(q_{3})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{3})
:

\textstyle{\boldsymbol{\omega}(q_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\dot{\chi}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\overline{C_{+}(\alpha)}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{+}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{2})}

\textstyle{\boldsymbol{\omega}(q_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\dot{\chi}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\overline{C_{-}(\alpha)}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{-}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{3})}

Therefore,

(3.16)

\overline{C_{+}(\alpha)}=\theta_{+}\circ\dot{\chi}^{\prime}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\overline{C_{-}(\alpha)}=\theta_{-}\circ\dot{\chi}^{\prime}.

Next, define a Lie algebra homomorphism

\Psi_{\mathcal{C}}\colon\boldsymbol{\omega}(q_{1})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathcal{C})

by composing
the differential
\dot{\psi}_{\End(\mathcal{C})}\colon\boldsymbol{\omega}(q_{1})\to\mathfrak{go}(\mathcal{C})
of the morphism
\psi_{\End(\mathcal{C})}\colon\BOmega(q_{1})\to\BGO(\mathcal{C})
of §
3.2
with the canonical map

\mathfrak{go}(\mathcal{C})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathcal{C})
. Explicitly,

\Psi_{\mathcal{C}}(\xi)=(\dot{\chi}_{0}(\xi)+F,\;C_{+}(\alpha)(\xi)+F,\;C_{-}(\alpha)(\xi)+F)\qquad\text{for $\xi\in\boldsymbol{\omega}(q_{1})$,}

or, since
Proposition
1.15
shows that
\dot{\chi}^{\prime}(\xi)=\dot{\chi}_{0}(\xi)+F
,

\Psi_{\mathcal{C}}(\xi)=\bigl(\dot{\chi}^{\prime}(\xi),\;\overline{C_{+}(\alpha)}(\xi),\;\overline{C_{-}(\alpha)}(\xi)\bigr)\qquad\text{for $\xi\in\boldsymbol{\omega}(q_{1})$.}

It follows
from the definitions that the following diagrams are commutative:

\textstyle{\boldsymbol{\omega}(q_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Psi_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\dot{\chi}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\overline{C_{+}(\alpha)}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{1})}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{2})}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{2}}

\textstyle{\boldsymbol{\omega}(q_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Psi_{\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\dot{\chi}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\overline{C_{-}(\alpha)}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{1})}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{3})}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{3}}

Therefore,

\overline{C_{+}(\alpha)}=\pi_{2}\circ\Psi_{\mathcal{C}},\qquad\dot{\chi}^{\prime}=\pi_{1}\circ\Psi_{\mathcal{C}},\qquad\overline{C_{-}(\alpha)}=\pi_{3}\circ\Psi_{\mathcal{C}}.

Substituting in (
3.16
) yields

\pi_{2}\circ\Psi_{\mathcal{C}}=\theta_{+}\circ\pi_{1}\circ\Psi_{\mathcal{C}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\theta_{-}\circ\pi_{1}\circ\Psi_{\mathcal{C}}=\pi_{3}\circ\Psi_{\mathcal{C}}.

We know from Proposition
1.15
that
\dot{\chi}^{\prime}
is
surjective, hence
\Psi_{\mathcal{C}}
also is surjective since
\pi_{1}
is an
isomorphism. Therefore, the last displayed equations yield

\pi_{2}=\theta_{+}\circ\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{3}=\theta_{-}\circ\pi_{1}
, proving the
commutativity of diagram (
3.15
). Bijectivity of

\theta_{+}
and
\theta_{-}
follows, since

\theta_{+}=\pi_{2}\circ\pi_{1}^{-1}
and
\theta_{-}=\pi_{3}\circ\pi_{1}^{-1}
.
∎

We next apply Lemma
3.15
to the derived compositions

\partial\mathcal{C}
and
\partial^{2}\mathcal{C}
. Recall from (
2.20
)
and (
2.21
) the trialitarian triples

\End(\partial\mathcal{C})=\bigl((\End(V_{2}),\sigma_{b_{2}},\strf_{q_{2}}),\,(\End(V_{3}),\sigma_{b_{3}},\strf_{q_{3}}),\,(\End(V_{1}),\sigma_{b_{1}},\strf_{q_{1}}),\,C_{0}(\alpha^{\prime})\bigr)

and

\End(\partial^{2}\mathcal{C})=\bigl((\End(V_{3}),\sigma_{b_{3}},\strf_{q_{3}}),\,(\End(V_{1}),\sigma_{b_{1}},\strf_{q_{1}}),\,(\End(V_{2}),\sigma_{b_{2}},\strf_{q_{2}}),\,C_{0}(\alpha^{\prime\prime})\bigr).

Let

\theta^{\prime}_{+}\colon\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{2})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{3})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\theta^{\prime}_{-}\colon\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{2})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{1})

be the Lie algebra isomorphisms induced by
C_{+}(\alpha^{\prime})
and

C_{-}(\alpha^{\prime})
respectively, and

\theta^{\prime\prime}_{+}\colon\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{3})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{1})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\theta^{\prime\prime}_{-}\colon\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{3})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{2})

those induced by
C_{+}(\alpha^{\prime\prime})
and
C_{-}(\alpha^{\prime\prime})
. Let also

\pi^{\prime}_{1}\colon\mathfrak{pgo}(\partial\mathcal{C})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{2}),\qquad\pi^{\prime}_{2}\colon\mathfrak{pgo}(\partial\mathcal{C})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{3}),\qquad\pi^{\prime}_{3}\colon\mathfrak{pgo}(\partial\mathcal{C})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{1})

and

\pi^{\prime\prime}_{1}\colon\mathfrak{pgo}(\partial^{2}\mathcal{C})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{3}),\qquad\pi^{\prime\prime}_{2}\colon\mathfrak{pgo}(\partial^{2}\mathcal{C})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{1}),\qquad\pi^{\prime\prime}_{3}\colon\mathfrak{pgo}(\partial^{2}\mathcal{C})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{2})

be the projections on the various components of
\mathfrak{pgo}(\partial\mathcal{C})

and
\mathfrak{pgo}(\partial^{2}\mathcal{C})
. Lemma
3.15
yields

(3.17)

\theta^{\prime}_{+}=\pi^{\prime}_{2}\circ{\pi^{\prime}_{1}}^{-1},\qquad\theta^{\prime}_{-}=\pi^{\prime}_{3}\circ{\pi^{\prime}_{1}}^{-1},\qquad\theta^{\prime\prime}_{+}=\pi^{\prime\prime}_{2}\circ{\pi^{\prime\prime}_{1}}^{-1},\qquad\theta^{\prime\prime}_{-}=\pi^{\prime\prime}_{3}\circ{\pi^{\prime\prime}_{1}}^{-1}.

Proposition 3.16
.

The following equations hold:

\theta^{\prime}_{+}=\theta_{-}\circ\theta_{+}^{-1},\qquad\theta^{\prime}_{-}=\theta_{+}^{-1},\qquad\theta^{\prime\prime}_{+}=\theta_{-}^{-1},\qquad\theta^{\prime\prime}_{-}=\theta_{+}\circ\theta_{-}^{-1}.

Proof.

The switch maps
\partial
fit in the following
commutative diagrams:

\textstyle{\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{pgo}(\partial\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\prime}_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{1})}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{pgo}(\partial^{2}\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\prime\prime}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\partial}

\textstyle{\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{2}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{pgo}(\partial\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\prime}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{2})}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{pgo}(\partial^{2}\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\prime\prime}_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\partial}

and

\textstyle{\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{3}}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{pgo}(\partial\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\prime}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{pgo}(q_{3})}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{pgo}(\partial^{2}\mathcal{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\prime\prime}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\partial}

Substituting
\pi^{\prime}_{1}=\pi_{2}\circ\partial^{2}
,

\pi^{\prime}_{2}=\pi_{3}\circ\partial^{2}
,
\pi^{\prime}_{3}=\pi_{1}\circ\partial^{2}
and

\pi^{\prime\prime}_{1}=\pi_{3}\circ\partial
,
\pi^{\prime\prime}_{2}=\pi_{1}\circ\partial
,

\pi^{\prime\prime}_{3}=\pi_{2}\circ\partial
in (
3.17
) yields

\theta^{\prime}_{+}=\pi_{3}\circ\pi_{2}^{-1},\qquad\theta^{\prime}_{-}=\pi_{1}\circ\pi_{2}^{-1},\qquad\theta^{\prime\prime}_{+}=\pi_{1}\circ\pi_{3}^{-1},\qquad\theta^{\prime\prime}_{-}=\pi_{2}\circ\pi_{3}^{-1}.

The proposition follows by Lemma
3.15
.
∎

The maps
\theta_{\pm}
,
\theta^{\prime}_{\pm}
,
\theta^{\prime\prime}_{\pm}
thus fit in the
following commutative diagram, in which all the maps are isomorphisms:

(3.18)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 15.87323pt\raise-49.6265pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.98112pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\theta^{\prime\prime}_{+}}$}}}\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 79.24487pt\raise-40.40155pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.3978pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\theta^{\prime\prime}_{-}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 119.69891pt\raise-8.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 122.28485pt\raise-89.75pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Proof of Theorem
3.14
.

Corollary
1.22
shows that it suffices to prove the
claim after a Galois scalar extension that splits the trialitarian
triple
\mathcal{T}
. We may thus assume that
\mathcal{T}=\End(\mathcal{C})
for some
composition
\mathcal{C}
of quadratic spaces of dimension
8
. Then
Proposition
3.16
shows that

\theta_{-}\circ\theta_{+}^{-1}
and
\theta_{+}^{-1}
(resp.
\theta_{-}^{-1}
and
\theta_{+}\circ\theta_{-}^{-1}
) are the Lie algebra
homomorphisms induced by the isomorphisms
C_{+}(\alpha^{\prime})
and

C_{-}(\alpha^{\prime})
(resp.
C_{+}(\alpha^{\prime\prime})
and
C_{-}(\alpha^{\prime\prime})
) of the
trialitarian triple
\End(\partial\mathcal{C})
(resp.
\End(\partial^{2}\mathcal{C})
), hence they are liftable by
definition. Moreover,
\theta_{-}\circ\theta_{+}^{-1}
and

\theta_{+}^{-1}
(resp.
\theta_{-}^{-1}
,

\theta_{+}\circ\theta_{-}^{-1}
) are of opposite signs, hence the proof
is complete.
∎

Definition 3.17
.

Given any trialitarian triple

\mathcal{T}=(\mathfrak{A}_{1},\,\mathfrak{A}_{2},\,\mathfrak{A}_{3},\,\varphi_{0})
with Lie algebra
isomorphisms

\theta_{+}\colon\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathfrak{A}_{2})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\theta_{-}\colon\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\to\mathfrak{pgo}(\mathfrak{A}_{3})

induced by the components
\varphi_{+}\colon\mathfrak{C}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\to\mathfrak{A}_{2}

and
\varphi_{-}\colon\mathfrak{C}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\to\mathfrak{A}_{3}
of
\varphi_{0}
, the pair
of opposite Lie algebra isomorphisms
(\theta^{\prime}_{+},\,\theta^{\prime}_{-})=(\theta_{-}\circ\theta_{+}^{-1},\,\theta_{+}^{-1})
(resp.
(\theta^{\prime\prime}_{+},\,\theta^{\prime\prime}_{-})=(\theta_{-}^{-1},\,\theta_{+}\circ\theta_{-}^{-1})
)
lifts by
Theorem
3.14
to an isomorphism

\varphi_{0}^{\prime}\colon\mathfrak{C}(\mathfrak{A}_{2})\to\mathfrak{A}_{3}\times\mathfrak{A}_{1}\qquad\qquad\text{(resp.\ $\varphi_{0}^{\prime\prime}\colon\mathfrak{C}(\mathfrak{A}_{3})\to\mathfrak{A}_{1}\times\mathfrak{A}_{2}$)}

that defines a trialitarian triple

\partial\mathcal{T}=(\mathfrak{A}_{2},\,\mathfrak{A}_{3},\,\mathfrak{A}_{1},\,\varphi_{0}^{\prime})\qquad\qquad\text{(resp.\ $\partial^{2}\mathcal{T}=(\mathfrak{A}_{3},\,\mathfrak{A}_{1},\,\mathfrak{A}_{2},\,\varphi^{\prime\prime}_{0})$)}.

The trialitarian triples
\partial\mathcal{T}
and
\partial^{2}\mathcal{T}
are
called the
derived trialitarian triples
of
\mathcal{T}
.

Note that
\theta^{\prime}_{-}\circ{\theta^{\prime}_{+}}^{-1}=\theta^{\prime\prime}_{+}
and

{\theta^{\prime}_{+}}^{-1}=\theta_{-}^{\prime\prime}
, hence
\partial(\partial\mathcal{T})=\partial^{2}\mathcal{T}
. Similarly,
\partial^{2}(\partial\mathcal{T})=\mathcal{T}=\partial(\partial^{2}\mathcal{T})
and
\partial^{2}(\partial^{2}\mathcal{T})=\partial\mathcal{T}
.

From the proof of Theorem
3.14
, it is clear that
for every composition
\mathcal{C}
of quadratic
spaces of dimension
8
,

\partial\End(\mathcal{C})=\End(\partial\mathcal{C})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\partial^{2}\End(\mathcal{C})=\End(\partial^{2}\mathcal{C}).

We next establish the functoriality of the
\partial
operation.

Proposition 3.18
.

If
\gamma=(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3})\colon\mathcal{T}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}
is
an isomorphism of trialitarian triples, then

\partial\gamma:=(\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3},\gamma_{1})
is an isomorphism of
trialitarian triples
\partial\mathcal{T}\to\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}
.

Proof.

Let
(\theta_{+},\theta_{-})
(resp.
(\widetilde{\theta}_{+},\widetilde{\theta}_{-})
) be the pair of liftable
homomorphisms attached to
\mathcal{T}
(resp.
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}
). The
hypothesis that
\gamma
is an isomorphism means that

\gamma_{2}\circ\theta_{+}=\widetilde{\theta}_{+}\circ\gamma_{1}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\gamma_{3}\circ\theta_{-}=\widetilde{\theta}_{-}\circ\gamma_{1}.

It then follows that

\gamma_{3}\circ(\theta_{-}\circ\theta_{+}^{-1})=\widetilde{\theta}_{-}\circ\gamma_{1}\circ\theta_{+}^{-1}=(\widetilde{\theta}_{-}\circ\widetilde{\theta}_{+}^{-1})\circ\gamma_{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\gamma_{1}\circ\theta_{+}^{-1}=\widetilde{\theta}_{+}^{-1}\circ\gamma_{2}.

Since
(\theta_{-}\circ\theta_{+}^{-1},\,\theta_{+}^{-1})
and

(\widetilde{\theta}_{-}\circ\widetilde{\theta}_{+}^{-1},\,\widetilde{\theta}_{+}^{-1})
are the pairs of
liftable homomorphisms attached to
\partial\mathcal{T}
and

\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}
respectively, it follows that

(\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3},\gamma_{1})
is an isomorphism

\partial\mathcal{T}\to\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}
.
∎

For the next corollary, observe that each trialitarian triple

\mathcal{T}=(\mathfrak{A}_{1},\,\mathfrak{A}_{2},\,\mathfrak{A}_{3},\,\varphi_{0})
yields a polarization of

\mathfrak{A}_{1}
in the sense of Definition
1.4
: the
primitive idempotents in the center of
C(\mathfrak{A}_{1})
are designated as

z_{1+}
and
z_{1-}
according to the following convention:

\varphi_{0}(z_{1+})=(1,0)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\varphi_{0}(z_{1-})=(0,1),

so that the two components of
\varphi_{0}
are
\varphi_{+}\colon\mathfrak{C}_{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{A}_{2}
and
\varphi_{-}\colon\mathfrak{C}_{-}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathfrak{A}_{3}
. Similarly, the maps

\varphi_{0}^{\prime}
and
\varphi_{0}^{\prime\prime}
of the derived trialitarian triples

\partial\mathcal{T}
and
\partial^{2}\mathcal{T}
yield polarizations of
\mathfrak{A}_{2}
and

\mathfrak{A}_{3}
so that

\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(z_{2+})=(1,0),\quad\varphi^{\prime}_{0}(z_{2-})=(0,1),\qquad\varphi^{\prime\prime}_{0}(z_{3+})=(1,0),\quad\varphi^{\prime\prime}_{0}(z_{3-})=(0,1),

just as in the case of compositions of quadratic spaces: see
Remark
2.10
.

Corollary 3.19
.

Let
\mathcal{T}=(\mathfrak{A}_{1},\,\mathfrak{A}_{2},\,\mathfrak{A}_{3},\,\varphi_{0})
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}=(\widetilde{\mathfrak{A}}_{1},\,\widetilde{\mathfrak{A}}_{2},\,\widetilde{\mathfrak{A}}_{3},\,\widetilde{\varphi}_{0})
be
trialitarian triples. There are canonical one-to-one correspondences
between the following sets:

(i)

isomorphisms of trialitarian triples
\mathcal{T}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}
;

(ii)

isomorphisms of algebras with quadratic pair

\mathfrak{A}_{1}\to\widetilde{\mathfrak{A}}_{1}
preserving the polarizations induced by

\mathcal{T}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}
;

(iii)

isomorphisms of algebras with quadratic pair

\mathfrak{A}_{2}\to\widetilde{\mathfrak{A}}_{2}
preserving the polarizations induced by

\partial\mathcal{T}
and
\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}
;

(iv)

isomorphisms of algebras with quadratic pair

\mathfrak{A}_{3}\to\widetilde{\mathfrak{A}}_{3}
preserving the polarizations induced by

\partial^{2}\mathcal{T}
and
\partial^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}
.

Proof.

By definition, an isomorphism
\gamma\colon\mathcal{T}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}
is a
triple
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3})
where each
\gamma_{i}
is an
isomorphism
\mathfrak{A}_{i}\to\widetilde{\mathfrak{A}}_{i}
and the following square
commutes:

(3.19)

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 26.57936pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-16.49309pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 20.9118pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\varphi_{0}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.49309pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-26.57936pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{C(\gamma_{1})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 40.49309pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathfrak{A}_{2}\times\mathfrak{A}_{3}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 59.36813pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.48613pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\gamma_{2}\times\gamma_{3}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 59.36813pt\raise-24.27777pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-15.52086pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}(\widetilde{\mathfrak{A}}_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.25pt\raise-24.63889pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.86111pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 42.43753pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 42.43753pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathfrak{A}}_{2}\times\widetilde{\mathfrak{A}}_{3}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces

Commutativity of this square implies that
\gamma_{1}
preserves the
polarizations of
\mathfrak{A}_{1}
and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{A}}_{1}
induced by
\mathcal{T}
and

\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}
.

Conversely, if
\gamma_{1}\colon\mathfrak{A}_{1}\to\widetilde{\mathfrak{A}}_{1}
is an
isomorphism of algebras with quadratic pair preserving
polarizations, then there are isomorphisms

\gamma_{2}\colon\mathfrak{A}_{2}\to\widetilde{\mathfrak{A}}_{2}
and
\gamma_{3}\colon\mathfrak{A}_{3}\to\widetilde{\mathfrak{A}}_{3}
uniquely determined by the condition that the
square (
3.19
) commute. The triple

(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3})
is then an isomorphism

\mathcal{T}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}
. Thus, the sets described in (i) and (ii) are
in bijection under the map carrying

\gamma=(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3})
to
\gamma_{1}
. Similarly, the
set in (iii) is in bijection with the set of isomorphisms

\partial\mathcal{T}\to\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}
, hence, by
Proposition
3.18
, with the set of isomorphisms

\mathcal{T}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}
: to each isomorphism

\gamma\colon\mathcal{T}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}
corresponds the second component

\gamma_{2}\colon\mathfrak{A}_{2}\to\widetilde{\mathfrak{A}}_{2}
. Likewise, mapping
\gamma
to

\gamma_{3}
defines a one-to-one correspondence between (i) and (iv).
∎

In the particular case where
\widetilde{\mathcal{T}}=\mathcal{T}
,
Corollary
3.19
yields isomorphisms between the group of
automorphisms of
\mathcal{T}
and the groups of polarization-preserving
automorphisms of
\mathfrak{A}_{1}
,
\mathfrak{A}_{2}
and
\mathfrak{A}_{3}
, which are

\PGO^{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})
,
\PGO^{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{2})
and
\PGO^{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{3})
. We discuss
this case in detail in the next subsection.

3.4.
Trialitarian isomorphisms

Throughout this subsection, we fix a trialitarian triple

\mathcal{T}=(\mathfrak{A}_{1},\,\mathfrak{A}_{2},\,\mathfrak{A}_{3},\,\varphi_{0})
. We show how to attach to

\mathcal{T}
canonical isomorphisms, which we call
trialitarian
isomorphisms
:

\BSpin(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\simeq\BSpin(\mathfrak{A}_{2})\simeq\BSpin(\mathfrak{A}_{3})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\BPGO^{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{1})\simeq\BPGO^{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{2})\simeq\BPGO^{+}(\mathfrak{A}_{3}).

Proposition
3.18
shows that the switch map
\partial

yields an isomorphism

\partial\colon\BGO(\mathcal{T})\to\BGO(\partial\mathcal{T}).

This isomorphism maps
\BHomot(\mathcal{T})
to
\BHomot(\partial\mathcal{T})
, hence
it induces a switch isomorphism
\partial\colon\BPGO(\mathcal{T})\to\BPGO(\partial\mathcal{T})
. The following proposition shows that
\partial

also maps
\BOrth(\mathcal{T})
to
\BOrth(\partial\mathcal{T})
:

Proposition 3.20
.

The following diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{\BGO(\mathcal{T})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{\mathcal{T}}}
\textstyle{\BGO(\partial\mathcal{T})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{\partial\mathcal{T}}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{m}}^{3}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\textstyle{\mathbf{G}_{\mathbf{m}}^{3}}

Proof.

When
\mathcal{T}=\End(\mathcal{C})
for some composition
\mathcal{C}
of quadratic
spaces of dimension
8
, then
\BGO(\mathcal{T})=\BGO(\mathcal{C})
and

\BGO(\partial\mathcal{T})=\BGO(\partial\mathcal{C})
by
Proposition
3.10
, and commutativity of the diagram
is clear from (
3.13
). Commutativity for an
arbitrary
trialitarian triple follows by scalar extension to a splitting
field.
∎

Recall from Corollary
3.13
the

\partial\mathcal{T}

\mathcal{T}

\mathfrak{A}_{1}
and
\mathcal{T}
